    window.loadScript = (url, { charset = 'UTF-8', ...attributes } = {}, location = 'head') => {
      const existingScript = document.querySelector('script[src="' + url + '"]');
      if (existingScript) {
        return;
      }

      const script = document.createElement('script');
      script.src = url;
      script.type = 'text/javascript';

      script.async = true;
      script.charset = charset;

      if (attributes) {
        Object.entries(attributes).forEach(([key, value]) => {
          script.setAttribute(key, value);
        });
      }

      if (location === 'head') {
        document.head.appendChild(script);
      } else {
        document.body.appendChild(script);
      }

      return script;
    };

uid="Zmkvd2" component-url="/_astro/GoogleAnalytics.Buaiy_s2.js" component-export="GoogleAnalytics" renderer-url="/_astro/client.CpQ9otmg.js" props="{&quot;title&quot;:[0,&quot;Why we cannot wait for better post-quantum signature algorithms&quot;],&quot;canonical&quot;:[0,&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/ml-dsa-will-have-to-do&quot;],&quot;info&quot;:[0,{&quot;id&quot;:[0,&quot;5wMIF1Oa7DES2YNRpqiVB0&quot;],&quot;title&quot;:[0,&quot;Why we cannot wait for better post-quantum signature algorithms&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;ml-dsa-will-have-to-do&quot;],&quot;excerpt&quot;:[0,&quot;NIST is advancing nine new post-quantum signature algorithms as potential candidates for future standardization. We take a closer look at all of them, and argue that while they are in the works and show great potential, we should use ML-DSA for now â the best one currently available. &quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;RSA and ECC, cryptographic algorithms that weâve all relied on for decades, are &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/the-quantum-menace/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;vulnerable&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to the attack of sufficiently advanced quantum computers. Such quantum computers do not exist yet, but they seem to be coming &lt;a href=\&quot;https://scottaaronson.blog/?p=9718\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;sooner&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; than expected. Luckily, the solution is already available: migrate to ML-KEM encryption and ML-DSA signatures, which are designed to be resistant to quantum attack. They were standardized &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/nists-first-post-quantum-standards/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;in 2024&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; by the U.S. National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST) after an eight-year open international competition.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The migration to post-quantum cryptography is in full swing now. At the time of writing, the majority of traffic handled by Cloudflare is already using ML-KEM encryption, and is thus secured against the threat to data posed by &lt;a href=\&quot;https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvest_now,_decrypt_later\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;harvest-now-decrypt-later&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; attacks. But encryption is only one part of the equation: to be fully secure against quantum computers capable of breaking classical cryptography, we aim to deploy post-quantum signatures to protect authentication systems from unauthorized access. We are &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/post-quantum-roadmap\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;targeting 2029&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for Cloudflare to be fully post-quantum secure.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;ML-DSA, the best all-around post-quantum signature scheme standardized today, has its downsides: itâs much larger on the wire, and many &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/fancy-cryptography/fancy-cryptography\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;tricks&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; we were able to perform with RSA and ECC simply cannot be done with ML-DSA. There are better post-quantum signature schemes on the horizon: last month, NIST &lt;a href=\&quot;https://groups.google.com/a/list.nist.gov/g/pqc-forum/c/LXoTAe5AN78/m/ZXgBCNlgDAAJ\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;announced&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; that it is advancing nine post-quantum signature schemes to the third round of the â&lt;a href=\&quot;https://csrc.nist.gov/projects/pqc-dig-sig\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;signatures on-ramp&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;â. And a draft standard for &lt;a href=\&quot;https://falcon-sign.info/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;FN-DSA&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; (nÃ©e Falcon), which was picked from the previous competition, is expected imminently.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We have been very interested in advances in post-quantum signature algorithms, and wrote about the progress in &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/sizing-up-post-quantum-signatures/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;2021&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/nist-post-quantum-surprise/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;2022&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/another-look-at-pq-signatures/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;2024&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/pq-2025/#signatures-on-the-horizon\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;2025&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. In this blog post weâll treat you to the latest developments in great detail.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;But first we have to deal with the elephant in the room: These new signature algorithms will not be ready in time for the PQ transition â not even close, as we &lt;a href=\&quot;#timelines\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;will see later on&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. The problem is arriving too soon for us to wait. ML-DSA is available today, and it will have to do for the first migration. As Eric Rescorla &lt;a href=\&quot;https://educatedguesswork.org/posts/pq-emergency/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;wrote&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; in 2024:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;blockquote&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;i&gt;You go to war with the algorithms you have, not the ones you wish you had.&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/blockquote&gt;&lt;p&gt;Nonetheless, the search for better post-quantum signature algorithms is crucial for several reasons, and we firmly believe it is still the best use of NISTâs limited resources.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Letâs have a look at the signature algorithms in detail. After that weâll look at the timeline for their availability, and the reasons why we still need them.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;the-signature-algorithms\&quot;&gt;The signature algorithms&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-signature-algorithms\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;In the table below, we compare the candidate signature algorithms that progressed to the third round (marked by ð¤), with classical algorithms vulnerable to quantum attack (marked by â), and the post-quantum algorithms that are already standardized ( â) or soon will be (ð).
