Locale&quot;]}],&quot;url&quot;:[0,&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/ml-dsa-will-have-to-do&quot;],&quot;metadata&quot;:[0,{&quot;title&quot;:[0,&quot;Why we cannot wait for better post-quantum signature algorithms&quot;],&quot;description&quot;:[0,&quot;NIST is advancing nine new post-quantum signature algorithms as potential candidates for future standardization. We take a closer look at all of them, and argue that while they are in the works and show great potential, we should use ML-DSA for now â the best one currently available. &quot;],&quot;imgPreview&quot;:[0,&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/7FnkyOWGzxzznog9Kqw3zV/7c747b631184511eed931057e5db3dd0/Why_we_cannot_wait_for_better_post-quantum_signature_algorithms-OG.png&quot;]}],&quot;publicly_index&quot;:[0,true]}],&quot;initialReadingTime&quot;:[0,&quot;25&quot;],&quot;relatedPosts&quot;:[1,[[0,{&quot;id&quot;:[0,&quot;7zbgYCOZDfvIIsVrPpZ8xJ&quot;],&quot;title&quot;:[0,&quot;Introducing Meerkat: an experiment in global consensus&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;meerkat-introduction&quot;],&quot;excerpt&quot;:[0,&quot;Cloudflare Research is building a global consensus service called Meerkat that uses a new consensus algorithm called QuePaxa. We plan to use Meerkat to build a strongly consistent, fault-tolerant key-value store, and other applications.&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;Many internal services at Cloudflare need to read and modify the same control-plane state from across our 330+ global data centers. They need guarantees that different readers &lt;i&gt;never &lt;/i&gt;see inconsistent state, and that the system remains available for writes even when some data centers or links fail. &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;But Cloudflareâs network runs across the entire Internet, and the Internet is an unpredictable place. Servers and data centers go down. Queues fill up. Links and cables get cut. These conditions make it difficult to run a globally available data system that guarantees strong consistency (e.g., that all readers are guaranteed to read all prior writes) because hostile conditions hinder distributed system replicasâ ability to reliably synchronize data with one another.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;One way to synchronize data safely despite adverse network conditions is via a &lt;i&gt;consensus algorithm, &lt;/i&gt;which&lt;i&gt; &lt;/i&gt;allows a set of machines to agree on the same sequence of values, such as key-value store put and &lt;code&gt;get&lt;/code&gt; operations, as long as a majority remains alive and able to communicate.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Unfortunately, commonly deployed consensus algorithms like &lt;a href=\&quot;https://raft.github.io/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Raft&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; suffer in wide-area networks like Cloudflareâs because they rely on &lt;i&gt;leaders &lt;/i&gt;and&lt;i&gt; timeouts&lt;/i&gt;. The &lt;i&gt;leader&lt;/i&gt; is the only replica allowed to make writes, and if it fails due to a crash or network degradation, the system becomes unavailable until some other replica &lt;i&gt;times out&lt;/i&gt; and a new leader is elected. And these timeout values are hard to configure in networks with unpredictable latencies.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We have experienced multiple incidents caused by unavailable leaders in consensus-driven systems.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;And so, for the past year, Cloudflareâs Research &lt;a href=\&quot;https://research.cloudflare.com/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;team&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; has been building a new distributed consensus service called &lt;b&gt;Meerkat&lt;/b&gt; powered by a consensus algorithm called &lt;a href=\&quot;https://bford.info/pub/os/quepaxa/quepaxa.pdf\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;QuePaxa&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, published in 2023 by Tennage &amp;amp; BÄsescu et al. QuePaxa differs from Raft in that all replicas can perform writes at all times, and progress is never halted due to a timeout, which makes it well suited for Cloudflareâs network. We layer &lt;i&gt;applications&lt;/i&gt;, like a transactional key-value store and leasing system, atop Meerkatâs consensus log. To our knowledge, this will be the first industrial deployment of QuePaxa at global scale.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Meerkat is an experimental consensus service that is still in development. Itâs being designed initially to manage small pieces of control plane state (e.g., leadership for replicated databases) and so it will be kept internal-only for the immediate future. This post introduces Meerkat and lays the groundwork for the Meerkat-related blog posts to come.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;what-we-need-from-a-global-control-plane-data-system\&quot;&gt;What we need from a global control-plane data system&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-we-need-from-a-global-control-plane-data-system\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Many Cloudflare services read and write &lt;i&gt;control-plane data&lt;/i&gt;, data that helps those services operate correctly, from multiple machines distributed all over the world. One example of control-plane data is &lt;i&gt;placement information&lt;/i&gt;: where certain resources (like an AI model instance) are stored. Another example is &lt;i&gt;leadership information&lt;/i&gt;: which machine is currently allowed to perform writes to a database.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Control-plane data must be both &lt;i&gt;strongly&lt;/i&gt; &lt;i&gt;consistent&lt;/i&gt; and&lt;i&gt; accessible despite particular kinds of faults.&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In this section we precisely describe our consistency and fault tolerance requirements for a Cloudflare consensus service. We use a key-value store for a running example of an application running atop our consensus service, though other applications (e.g., distributed leases/locks) are possible.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;strong-consistency\&quot;&gt;Strong consistency&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#strong-consistency\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;A distributed data systemâs &lt;a href=\&quot;https://jepsen.io/consistency/models\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;consistency&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; level describes what kinds of weird behavior the system is allowed to exhibit when it receives concurrent reads and writes. Consider a distributed key-value store that stores a single numeric value &lt;code&gt;x = 6&lt;/code&gt; across multiple nodes. Also consider the following sequence of writes. These writes are submitted to different nodes on a best-effort basis, and could arrive in any order:Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;x = x + 1&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;x = x / 2&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;&lt;p&gt;A systemâs consistency level tells you what values of &lt;code&gt;x&lt;/code&gt; a client might see when reading &lt;code&gt;x&lt;/code&gt; after these writes. Consider the following sequence of operations and the possible execution orders under different consistency levels:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/Ie5OrNyD2MJVN47XewDiD/52d0c61577c39f026ed49d5035a8d7cd/BLOG-3347_image4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3347 image4\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1144\&quot; height=\&quot;940\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;In a weak consistency level, writes can be re-ordered. In a stronger consistency model, writes canât be reordered, but reads can. In the strongest possible consistency level, the operations are ordered exactly as they occurred in real time. This property is called &lt;i&gt;linearizability&lt;/i&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;At Cloudflare, many services want linearizability. Unlike weaker forms of consistency, linearizability relieves programmers from thinking about all the weird behaviors the data systems might exhibit. Instead, they can reason about the distributed system like they reason about local memory on a single-threaded machine: all reads after a write will see that write. For additional reading material on the dangers of weak consistency, check out this &lt;a href=\&quot;https://brooker.co.za/blog/2025/11/18/consistency.html\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;post&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; by Marc Brooker.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;(If youâre wondering, Meerkatâs key-value store also provides serializability, which weâll write about in a future post.)&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;fault-tolerance\&quot;&gt;Fault tolerance&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#fault-tolerance\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;A systemâs level of fault tolerance describes what kinds of faults the system can handle before catastrophes happen. Catastrophes are typically violations of properties the system aims to uphold, e.g., that two consecutive reads without an intervening write for the same key never see different values, or that the system remains available for writes. The faults include network failures or delays, machine crashes, and machine restarts. A system will typically explicitly handle some faults but not others (you canât handle all faults, as the universe could always reach heat-death). For example, some key-value stores might guarantee to remain available for writes as long as two-thirds of the machines in the system can communicate and donât crash, but make no promises if a machine is compromised and starts sending malicious messages.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Our desired fault tolerance properties are as follows:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;First&lt;/b&gt;, the data system should remain available for writes and reads from a client located in any of our data centers as long as the following are true:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;A majority of the machines in our system are alive and can communicate with one another. (Formally, we tolerate &lt;code&gt;f&lt;/code&gt; faults in a system of &lt;code&gt;2f + 1&lt;/code&gt; machines).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;The client can contact &lt;i&gt;any&lt;/i&gt; machine in the system that is connected to a majority of live machines.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;&lt;p&gt;This means that a single failed machine, or network degradation on a single link, does not affect availability of the system&lt;i&gt;. &lt;/i&gt;This property is not provided by Raft-based systems, as weâll see later.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Second&lt;/b&gt;, the data system remains &lt;i&gt;correct&lt;/i&gt; as long as no actor in the system is actively malicious (and, of course, there are no bugs). We define &lt;i&gt;correctness&lt;/i&gt; in terms of consensus &lt;i&gt;safety &lt;/i&gt;later, but loosely speaking this means no two up-to-date machines will ever disagree about the world (e.g., one thinks that &lt;code&gt;key1=1&lt;/code&gt; while another thinks that &lt;code&gt;key1=2&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To summarize, the system must remain correct even if machines crash, machines restart, networks fail or degrade, data centers go down, and more (though we, like Raft-based systems, do not handle &lt;a href=\&quot;https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_fault\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Byzantine faults&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;).&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;introducing-meerkat\&quot;&gt;Introducing Meerkat&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#introducing-meerkat\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Meerkat is a consensus service &lt;i&gt;upon which &lt;/i&gt;we can build applications that exhibit the above properties (strong consistency and fault tolerance) like a key-value (KV) store. To understand how Meerkat works, we first outline Meerkatâs general architecture, and then describe how Meerkatâs choice of consensus algorithm helps provide strong consistency and fault tolerance.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Developers of services using Meerkat request a &lt;i&gt;cluster&lt;/i&gt; of Meerkat &lt;i&gt;replicas&lt;/i&gt;. Each replica is connected to every other replica. Each replica participates in the consensus algorithm and can receive both reads and writes. The developer can specify which data centers are allowed to host their replicas, and Meerkat places them automatically.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To interact with their cluster, a developerâs client sends an application-specific request to any replica in the cluster. A single replica may host many kinds of applications, but the simplest one is a key-value store, so the simplest application-specific request type is a KV &lt;code&gt;get&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;put&lt;/code&gt;. The replica responds to the request with an application-specific response (e.g., the records requested with the &lt;code&gt;get&lt;/code&gt;). Note that KV reads (&lt;code&gt;get&lt;/code&gt;s) are guaranteed to read up-to-date information.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/2DHsJkxyuRlmVZUhg2WoPh/4e83ae8c01ce7758a2af8c90895475c8/BLOG-3347_image2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3347 image2\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1344\&quot; height=\&quot;928\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;meerkats-log\&quot;&gt;Meerkatâs log&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#meerkats-log\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Under the hood, the replica translates application requests (e.g., &lt;code&gt;get&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;put&lt;/code&gt;) into &lt;i&gt;log events&lt;/i&gt;. That replica distributes each log event to all other replicas using a consensus algorithm such that all replicas maintain the exact same log of events (in reality, a replica may lag behind, but shall never record different entries). These events are arbitrary â Meerkatâs core doesnât care whatâs in them. Meerkat &lt;i&gt;applications&lt;/i&gt; care about log event contents. Each Meerkat replica âhostsâ many Meerkat applications (e.g., key-value store) that read the log events and construct state. (Note that each replica belongs to exactly one cluster.)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For instance, the KV Meerkat application constructs an in-memory key-value store from the log events. So when a client sends a write like &lt;code&gt;put k1 v1&lt;/code&gt;, the receiving replica places that write into a log event and distributes it to all replicas. If someone else subsequently writes &lt;code&gt;put k1 v11&lt;/code&gt; to a different replica, this event is also distributed to all replicas. Since all functioning replicas have the same log, those replicas can apply the operations in the log in sequence to construct the exact same state. Note that &lt;code&gt;get&lt;/code&gt; requests also create distributed log events (for linearizability, as explained in the next section).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Here is an example of how a replicaâs KV store is updated as it receives log events:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/5jTmSTMFIi20yFDIQtVf4l/4b1871e87d46668ecaa401a306755f60/BLOG-3347_image3.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3347 image3\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1676\&quot; height=\&quot;984\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;how-meerkats-log-enables-strong-consistency\&quot;&gt;How Meerkatâs log enables strong consistency&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#how-meerkats-log-enables-strong-consistency\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Meerkat guarantees that if one client executes &lt;code&gt;put k1 v1&lt;/code&gt;, a second client subsequently executes &lt;code&gt;put k1 v11&lt;/code&gt;, and a third client subsequently executes &lt;code&gt;get k1&lt;/code&gt; (with a consistent read), they will always read &lt;code&gt;v11&lt;/code&gt;. It guarantees this even if each request is submitted to a different replica, and those replicas are distributed randomly across the world. This is linearizability. To see how Meerkat guarantees this, we must examine Meerkatâs log in more detail.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The Meerkat log is a sequence of slots. A slot is a box that can contain an event or not. A slot that contains an event is called a &lt;i&gt;decided &lt;/i&gt;slot. All slots in the log are decided except the last slot, which is currently being decided. One of Meerkatâs invariants is that if any two replicas decide on the value for a slot, those values are the same. In other words, no two replicas will ever disagree on the value of a decided slot (though one replica may think the last slot is empty while another does not). This property helps guarantee the desired properties we described in the previous section.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To decide on the value of the last (empty) slot in the log, Meerkat replicas run a distributed &lt;i&gt;consensus algorithm&lt;/i&gt;. A consensus algorithm allows a set of machines communicating over a network to agree on a decided slot value. Our consensus algorithm works as long as a majority of replicas (more than half) are alive.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;So if the log currently contains two entries, and a client submits &lt;code&gt;put k1 v11&lt;/code&gt; to a replica, that replica triggers a consensus algorithm for slot 3. But another client might have submitted &lt;code&gt;put k1 v111&lt;/code&gt; to a different replica for slot 3. The consensus algorithm ensures that only one such &lt;i&gt;proposal&lt;/i&gt; for slot 3 wins out. Specifically, it ensures that at least a majority of replicas agree on the same proposal, &lt;i&gt;deciding &lt;/i&gt;it for slot 3. The non-majority can &lt;i&gt;never&lt;/i&gt; decide a different proposal, but might miss the fact that slot 3 has been decided at all.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/n4FcN0VCaSpM0jU1ptJJ2/77161cc5c179f10ff64a956ee10ab3b1/BLOG-3347_image5.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3347 image5\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1356\&quot; height=\&quot;881\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;To see how this provides linearizability for our key-value store, consider a write followed by a read. One replica Z proposes &lt;code&gt;put k1 v11&lt;/code&gt; and this proposal is decided at slot 3 by a majority of replicas, but NOT replica Y. Subsequently, a reader executes &lt;code&gt;get k1&lt;/code&gt; on replica Y. Replica Y believes slot 3 is empty, so proposes &lt;code&gt;get k1&lt;/code&gt; at slot 3. Critically, a majority of replicas will not agree to place that event at slot 3, because that slot has already been decided. They will force replica Y to decide (by receiving older decisions) &lt;code&gt;put k1 v11&lt;/code&gt; in slot 3, and to propose &lt;code&gt;get k1&lt;/code&gt; for slot 4, thus &lt;i&gt;linearizing &lt;/i&gt;the read after the write in the log. (And if that replica canât contact a majority, it will be unable to complete the read.)&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;how-meerkats-consensus-algorithm-provides-higher-availability-than-raft\&quot;&gt;How Meerkatâs consensus algorithm provides higher availability than Raft&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#how-meerkats-consensus-algorithm-provides-higher-availability-than-raft\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Deciding on log entries requires a distributed consensus algorithm. But which one? All valid consensus algorithms would provide the required consistency and correctness guarantees, but not all provide the same availability guarantees.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Specifically, many algorithms that rely on authoritative leaders do not provide our desired availability guarantees, because they can become unavailable when a single machine experiences issues. Consider &lt;a href=\&quot;https://raft.github.io/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Raft&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, one of the most well-known and probably &lt;i&gt;the&lt;/i&gt; most implemented consensus algorithm. Raft relies on an authoritative leader: the only replica in the cluster that can drive consensus. As a result, all writes get forwarded to the leader. This design choice helps make Raft â&lt;a href=\&quot;https://raft.github.io/raft.pdf\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;understandable&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;â and, coupled with &lt;a href=\&quot;https://dl.acm.org/doi/epdf/10.1145/3786663\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;leases&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, can make leader-served reads automatically linearizable (since theyâre guaranteed to be up-to-date). But it also adds a single point of (temporary) failure.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In general, there are two problems with authoritative leaders. First, if the leader goes down, the system becomes unavailable (all writes block) until a new leader is elected. This is unacceptable for Meerkat. Second, if the leader stays up but slows down, either because it is overloaded or there are network delays, then performance degrades. The leader is a bottleneck because there is no alternative way to perform writes.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The first problem is exacerbated in wide-area networks. Consider that when a leader goes down, most algorithms choose a new leader using &lt;i&gt;timeouts&lt;/i&gt;: if a non-leader replica hasnât heard from the leader in some amount of time, they propose themselves as the leader. At that point, the old leader has been deposed, and the system cannot accept writes until a new leader has been elected. The problem is that when the timeout is shorter than the network delay between the original leader and that replica, replicas will constantly be timing out and thus blocking writes. And when the timeout is too long, the system reacts slowly to a failed leader, during which writes are also blocked. Plus, if multiple replicas propose themselves as leader at the same time, their âcampaignsâ can interfere with each other, causing them to constantly re-propose themselves as leader â all the while blocking writes. We have seen these exact issues with Cloudflareâs systems that use Raft because our wide-area network delays can and do vary wildly, making tuning timeouts especially difficult.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We chose a different consensus algorithm for Meerkat, called &lt;a href=\&quot;https://bford.info/pub/os/quepaxa/quepaxa.pdf\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;QuePaxa&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, that aims to avoid the âtyranny of timeoutsâ imposed by protocols like Raft. QuePaxa is a subtle protocol, but here are the highlights. A client can contact &lt;i&gt;any &lt;/i&gt;replica, and that replica can drive consensus for the latest slot. There is a leader, but it is not required â its only advantage is that it can drive consensus with fewer round trips (one) than other replicas (3+). Critically, clients are free to contact multiple replicas &lt;i&gt;concurrently &lt;/i&gt;for the same proposal, to increase the chance of the proposal being successful. Concurrent proposals do not destructively interfere:Â  replicas &lt;i&gt;work together&lt;/i&gt; to decide one of the proposed values.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In short, QuePaxa has three advantages over Raft for our purposes:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Because there is no required leader, the system &lt;i&gt;never&lt;/i&gt; becomes unavailable or degraded due to a single replica (the leader) being down, unavailable, or degraded. Clients can perform writes as long as they can contact some healthy replica (anywhere in the world).Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Because there is no leader, there are no leader elections that degrade the system. And concurrent proposals made by different replicas &lt;i&gt;constructively &lt;/i&gt;interfere, unlike Raftâs leadership elections. This is ideal for Cloudflareâs network, in which latencies can vary wildly.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;QuePaxa was designed for a less reliable network environment (âasynchronyâ), and for networks in which an imaginary adversary can launch targeted attacks on replica connections. The authors found that it maintains much higher (~10x) throughput than Raft and Multi-Paxos during such conditions. These conditions more accurately resemble our own network than the conditions other algorithms assume.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;&lt;p&gt;We will save the full description of QuePaxa for another post. Major shoutout to the authors of the QuePaxa paper for being available for feedback and questions about their work.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;assessing-meerkats-performance\&quot;&gt;Assessing Meerkatâs performanceÂ &lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#assessing-meerkats-performance\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Meerkat has limitations. It is &lt;i&gt;not&lt;/i&gt; designed to create general-purpose data systems like databases.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;All consensus algorithms come with a cost: lots of round-trips. QuePaxa in particular takes one to three round trips (usually, although it can take more) between the initial proposer and a majority of replicas to decide on a proposal and add an event to the log. The difference is with the leader. It takes one if the leader is proposing (+ an extra broadcast to notify replicas of the decision) and three if a non-leader is proposing (+ extra broadcast). If multiple replicas make proposals at the same time, it can take more. These communication costs point to the important performance limitation of consensus algorithms in general: proposal decision latency is proportional to the latency between some majority of replicas. So if your replicas are far from one another, latency will increase â thereâs no getting around that.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;At first glance, it seems Meerkatâs write &lt;i&gt;and&lt;/i&gt; read latency will be quite poor. Especially if all writes and&lt;i&gt; &lt;/i&gt;reads&lt;i&gt; &lt;/i&gt;(for consistency) must go through the log, and thus require so many round trips.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;But there are a few ways to squeeze better performance out of Meerkat:Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Because developers have control over where their replicas live, they can choose to move replicas closer together, reducing round-trip latency (only applicable for services that donât need truly global distribution).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Writes can be batched. So if a replica receives 10 writes in a span of 10ms, it can place all of those in a single proposal, improving throughput&lt;i&gt;.&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Not all reads must trigger a consensus round. If a developer is OK with reading &lt;i&gt;stale &lt;/i&gt;(but never inconsistent) data, they can read from &lt;i&gt;any &lt;/i&gt;replicaâs local data.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Multiple operations can be bundled into a single consensus round. For instance, our key-value store supports compare-and-swap-style writes in which writes execute only if a value has not changed since it was read. (In fact, it supports general transactions.)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;&lt;p&gt;Still, Meerkatâs fundamental latency limitations remain, especially when it is run at global scale, as it was designed to do. These limitations make it perfect, in the short term, for control plane information that is written infrequently but must remain consistent.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;whats-next\&quot;&gt;Whatâs next&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#whats-next\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Meerkat is not deployed to production, but we have run multiple proofs-of-concept with up to 50 replicas distributed around the world, to great success. &lt;b&gt;Leaders in our proof-of-concept clusters constantly fail, and the cluster keeps operating with no increase in error-rate.&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We have a lot more to say about Meerkat. Over the course of the next year weâll be writing Meerkat posts that discuss how QuePaxa really works, how weâre formally verifying some of our Rust implementation, how bootstrapping and cluster management works, how we find optimal replica placement, how we use deterministic simulation testing to find bugs, and more. Weâll also be preparing a manuscript for peer-review!&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Follow along on the Cloudflare Blog as Meerkat progresses, and check out more of our projects at &lt;a Instead of approaching this framework as a new set of commitments to meet, we see it as a welcome validation from the UK government of the security philosophy and principles Cloudflare has championed for over a decade. We are glad to see the rest of the industry moving in this direction.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This pledge is an important step, and it comes at a time of significant cyber risk. In the first quarter of 2026, Cloudflare&amp;#39;s global network blocked an average of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/press/press-releases/2026/cloudflare-2026-threat-intelligence-report-nation-state-actors-and/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;234 billion cyber threats every day&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Recently, we mitigated a hyper-volumetric DDoS attack that peaked at &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/ddos-threat-report-2025-q4/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;31.4 Tbps&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. At the end of 2025, Cloudflare data showed that the UK had risen to be the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/ddos-threat-report-2025-q4/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;sixth-most targeted&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; location across the globe for DDoS attacks, with threat actors increasingly targeting application-layer services in financial services, aviation, and regional government infrastructure. This trend is consistent with broader data from the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.gov.uk/government/statistics/cyber-security-breaches-survey-20252026/cyber-security-breaches-survey-20252026#:~:text=Just%20over%20four%20in%20ten,UK%20charities%20%5Bfootnote%201%5D.\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;UK Cyber Security Breaches Survey&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, which revealed that 43% of surveyed British businesses and 28% of charities reported suffering from a cyber incident this past year.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;At the same time, frontier AI models are rapidly changing the security landscape, lowering the barrier to entry for attackers, and enabling more automated vulnerability scanning and more convincing phishing campaigns. Cloudflare has long been preparing for this shift. The defensive architecture we recently &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/frontier-model-defense/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;published&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for frontier cyber models reflects the same principle: security has to evolve as quickly as the threats companies face. Every layer of that harness architecture, from ML-based attack scoring to Zero Trust access controls, is available to Cloudflare customers today.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Against that backdrop, the pledge does something essential: it recognizes that collective defense is critical. It asks organizations to make cyber resilience a leadership-level priority, to implement appropriate controls to boost threat awareness, and to help ensure supply chains meet a meaningful security baseline. Most breaches still exploit well-understood gaps, like unpatched systems, weak access controls, or poor vendor oversight. Encouraging more organizations to close those gaps through enhanced governance, monitoring, and implementation is a necessary starting point.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare is fully aligned with the UK government&amp;#39;s mission to elevate cybersecurity governance within companies and organizations of all sizes. Every organization that raises its baseline makes the Internet safer for everyone else. Our mission at Cloudflare is to help build a better Internet, and we have always believed that cybersecurity and resilience work best when they are universal. A more resilient Internet is a better Internet.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;why-resilience-matters\&quot;&gt;Why resilience matters&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#why-resilience-matters\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/security/what-is-cyber-resilience/#:~:text=Unlike%20organizations%20that%20focus%20on,can%20cause%20when%20they%20happen.\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cyber resilience&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; is increasingly recognized as a core business requirement. Customers expect services to be available at all times, responsive, and trustworthy. And thatâs true even when the environment gets more challenging to operate in, whether from increased attacks, outages, abuse, or complexity.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Resilience ultimately is not just about recovering after something goes wrong. It is about designing security systems and operating models that can proactively track threat signals, seamlessly absorb disruptions, and adapt to be better. In this way, security and resilience are inseparable. Security controls are what make resilience real.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;how-cloudflare-helps-strengthen-resilience-through-security\&quot;&gt;How Cloudflare helps strengthen resilience through security&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#how-cloudflare-helps-strengthen-resilience-through-security\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Thanks to the scale of our network, we can help organizations build resilience by shifting protection closer to the edge, before threats reach core systems. We think about cyber resilience through a few core architectural principles:&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;security-as-a-default-not-a-product-tier\&quot;&gt;Security as a default, not a product tier&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#security-as-a-default-not-a-product-tier\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Cloudflare believes baseline security protections should be available to all and has been living that principle since our founding. We were the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/introducing-universal-ssl/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;first&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to offer SSL certificates, required for traffic encryption, to all users. We protect vulnerable voices through our Impact programs like &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/galileo/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Project Galileo&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/athenian/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Athenian Project&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. We continuously push the boundaries of Internet cryptography, including the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/post-quantum-crypto-should-be-free/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;deployment of post-quantum cryptography&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; across our network. Our &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/en-gb/plans/free/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;free plan&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; includes unmetered DDoS protection regardless of the size, duration, or volume of attacks, and also provides access to a global content delivery network (CDN) and DNSSEC. These capabilities have historically required expensive hardware and specialist security teams. But the pledgeâs aim of elevating organizational resilience and raising the cyber resilience floor across the UK economy only works if small businesses, local authorities, public services, and startups can afford to participate. Our model directly supports that goal.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;the-network-is-the-sensor\&quot;&gt;The network is the sensor&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-network-is-the-sensor\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Because Cloudflare directly peers with more than 13,000 networks globally, we see attack patterns as they emerge. Threat intelligence collected in one part of the network can be turned into protection everywhere else in a matter of seconds. A threat detected while mitigating an attack on a customer in Singapore can become a rule that helps protect a customer in Sheffield moments later. That same visibility also helps improve how we detect, score, and respond to attacks across Cloudflareâs network and security services. Visibility at scale leads to resilience at scale for Cloudflareâs customers and network.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;cloudflare-is-customer-zero\&quot;&gt;Cloudflare is customer zero&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#cloudflare-is-customer-zero\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Our customers benefit from the exact same industry-leading security products and infrastructure that safeguard our own systems. Cloudflare employees use Cloudflare Access and Gateway to reach internal applications, and every request to an internal system requires hard key-based multi-factor authentication, posture checks, and cryptographically verified identity tokens. We test every security layer on ourselves first, and use our own internal learnings to build better security solutions for ourselves and our network. By integrating security into every level of the business, Cloudflare demonstrates a ground-up commitment that sits at the very heart of the pledge.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;transparency-and-response\&quot;&gt;Transparency and response&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#transparency-and-response\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Finally, resilience requires honesty and transparency when things go wrong and a commitment to strengthen systems for the future. When &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/how-cloudflare-mitigated-yet-another-okta-compromise/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;security incidents&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; or zero-day vulnerabilities emerge, we publish deep-dive technical postmortems on the Cloudflare Blog. We share indicators of compromise and architectural retrospectives, so the broader security community can learn from our telemetry. But transparency is only the first step. We treat every incident as a mandate to make our network more resilient. After a significant outage last fall, our &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/code-orange-fail-small-complete/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Code Orange&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; effort mobilized engineering teams to rebuild for resilience. They designed systems to &amp;quot;fail small,&amp;quot; and built new tooling to enforce safer configuration changes and automate best practices, so the same failure can&amp;#39;t happen twice.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;how-cloudflare-implements-the-cyber-resilience-pledge-commitments\&quot;&gt;How Cloudflare implements the Cyber Resilience Pledge commitments&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#how-cloudflare-implements-the-cyber-resilience-pledge-commitments\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;As noted above, todayâs voluntary pledge asks companies and organizations to commit to certain standards in board responsibility and governance, supply chain security, and the technical requirements under the UKâs &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/trust-hub/compliance-resources/cyber-essentials/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cyber Essentials&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; certification scheme. As a global cybersecurity and network resilience provider, we operate an advanced internal cybersecurity governance model.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;board-responsibility-and-governance\&quot;&gt;Board responsibility and governance&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#board-responsibility-and-governance\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;With cybersecurity and resilience at the core of Cloudflare&amp;#39;s global business, we are proud to be a leader in &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/the-net/top-of-mind-technology/security-boardroom/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;developing and advocating for&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; practices that strengthen cybersecurity at the board level.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Our Board of Directors treats cyber risk oversight as a core responsibility. Cloudflareâs Board receives cybersecurity briefings from our Chief Security Officer on at least a quarterly basis, including direct threat briefings. In addition, the Audit Committee of the Board receives quarterly briefings on enterprise risk management that include a specific focus on cyber risks and the company&amp;#39;s process for regularly reviewing and mitigating cyber threats and risks.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We are grateful that DSIT&amp;#39;s toolkit and resources are available to benchmark, reinforce, and support boards&amp;#39; ongoing governance efforts across the entire UK economy.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;supply-chain-security-and-cyber-essentials-ce\&quot;&gt;Supply chain security and Cyber Essentials (CE)&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#supply-chain-security-and-cyber-essentials-ce\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Cloudflare adheres to rigorous international security compliance certifications. We require our supply chain to meet comprehensive international standards that incorporate and build upon the core requirements of Cyber Essentials. Cloudflare manages vendor risk globally, prioritizing comprehensive international security frameworks that encompass and exceed the fundamental technical controls of the Cyber Essentials program.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;More specifically, Cloudflare requires critical suppliers to adhere to rigorous, internationally recognized security compliance certifications and reports â primarily ISO 27001 and SOC 2 Type II. These frameworks explicitly require the implementation of firewalls, secure configurations, user access controls, malware protection, and patch management (the five core pillars of Cyber Essentials).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare will continue to use a risk-based methodology to evaluate suppliers. We commend DSIT for expanding access to the Cyber Essentials Supplier Check Tool, which Cloudflare can adopt for localized supply chain validation within the UK. And for global suppliers where UK Cyber Essentials is not a native or practical certification, Cloudflare will accept equivalent international certifications (like ISO 27001) as sufficient verification of a robust security posture. These practices help ensure that Cloudflare&amp;#39;s critical supply chain undergoes stringent security vetting, meeting the risk-reduction outcomes intended by Cyber Essentials.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;onward\&quot;&gt;Onward&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#onward\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Cyber resilience is not a one-time pledge â it is a continuous practice of building systems that fail safely, recover quickly, and learn to be better. For organizations across the UK, it means making cybersecurity a business-critical priority, with leadership buy-in, teams that understand the threats they face, and supply chains managed for risk. The pledge sets a baseline that every organization should strive to meet.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare built its platform on the belief that security and resilience should be universal and available to both the smallest developer and the largest enterprise. Imagine a world without it: we could find a thousand ways to regurgitate the same material thatâs already been created, but we would witness the decline of fresh ideas and arguments.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Website owners fuel the ecosystem of ideas, news, and interesting tidbits, but they face the increasingly complex challenge of managing traffic to their websites and being paid for their content. While some bot traffic is clearly malicious, it isnât always obvious when a particular AI crawler is helping or harming your business. To answer this, site owners need granular, reliable data to differentiate between traffic that provides value, and traffic that strains resources while eroding the foundation of their business model: actual humans consuming their content.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;At Cloudflare, we hold a core belief: website owners have the right to &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/content-independence-day-no-ai-crawl-without-compensation/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;control access to their content&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. We want to help website owners maintain their high-quality content and regulate AI traffic.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To provide much-needed clarity and help website owners take control, weâre excited to announce the new &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/attribution-business-insights/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Attribution Business Insights&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;u&gt; dashboard&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; â designed with business decision-makers and publishers in mind.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;the-new-economics-of-the-internet\&quot;&gt;The new economics of the Internet&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-new-economics-of-the-internet\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;For decades, the business model of the Internet relied on a straightforward, unspoken agreement: website owners allowed search engines to crawl their content and, in return, search engines sent readers back to their pages. This symbiotic relationship, where traditional search engines operated with a balanced &amp;quot;crawl-to-referral&amp;quot; ratio, generated the pageviews needed to sustain advertising, affiliate revenue, and subscriptions. Search index crawlers would scan your content &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/ai-search-crawl-refer-ratio-on-radar/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;a couple of times for each referral sent,&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; so making your website available to crawlers had a clear pipeline to additional revenue. We can think of this as the SEO (Search Engine Optimization) era.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, the explosive rise of AI crawlers and agents has broken this contract, plunging the digital publishing industry into an unprecedented crisis. The Internet is risking a transition into a &amp;quot;zero-click&amp;quot; ecosystem where AI chatbots scrape original content to synthesize instant answers â completely bypassing the original sources. Weâve already seen a marked shift from the SEO-only world into an AEO (Answer Engine Optimization) world, and now conversations around GEO (Generative Engine Optimization) are taking center stage.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The imbalance of this new reality is made clear by the crawl-to-referral ratios we see across the Internet today. While traditional search engines had a more balanced ratio of crawls to legitimate visitors referred, major AI crawlers operate on a drastically different, extractive scale. Bots from leading AI companies have been observed with a range of crawl-to-referral ratios: we noted ratios of 118:1 up to nearly 50,000:1 around the time of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/ai-crawler-traffic-by-purpose-and-industry/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;our Content Independence Day in 2025&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. In other words, an AI crawler might have crawled your premium content tens of thousands of times just to send back a single visitor. This ratio is fundamentally unfair.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For publishers, this creates a double hit: first, theyâre losing out on the crucial referral traffic, ad impressions, and direct audience relationships that fund content creation and journalism. Second, theyâre forced to bear the rising infrastructure costs of hosting and serving content to automated bots that offer no commercial value in return. The era in which it makes sense to allow &lt;b&gt;all&lt;/b&gt; crawlers in the hopes of being discovered is over.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;introducing-attribution-business-insights\&quot;&gt;Introducing Attribution Business Insights&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#introducing-attribution-business-insights\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;We want website owners to have the facts â the cold, hard numbers to understand which bots are helping their business and which bots are harming it.
